- Round 80 (20 November 2026): a fermata that was read as a note, and the one or two notes that keep three clean
  scores from being whole.

  The round turned from the scores that lack most to the scores that lack least: ten clean scores lack one to
  three notes, and a score counts for the milestone only when it is whole. Each of their places was looked at on
  the page. Four rules came of it. The first is a rule for every print and every hand; the three others are
  small, and two of them are rules for one case.

  - **A fermata is no note** (`fermatas.py`, a new module). The thick middle of a fermata's arc was read as a
    flat filled head, with an arm of the arc as its stem; under the staff the cup of the lower voice's fermata
    was read the same way. Such a note can take the place of a true one in the line-up of the count. A fermata
    is told by its whole shape, with no model: one piece of ink that is drawn through from end to end, about
    half as high as wide; its outer edge has dropped little a quarter of the way in and much at its two ends, by
    about as much at both; its top lies near its middle; both arms come down nearly to its open side; and a
    small solid dot stands under its top, in the hollow or just past the open side. The census is every piece of
    ink of LitChoir's scan pages with the size and the proportions of such an arc, no more than half of its box
    ink: 9,990 pieces on 485 pages, 452 of them with a dot at that place. The rule takes 214 of them, in print
    and by hand, and each was looked at: every one is a fermata. What stands close to its limits and is left
    out: a bracket by hand with a dot under it (it drops at once at its ends), a word by hand (no single
    stroke), a fermata that a scan cut at the edge of its page, and a fermata with a pencil line through it. A
    slur with a dot under it is longer and flatter. A head whose middle lies in a fermata is no note. Alone, on
    every scan page: 7 pages differ, 2 notes gained and none lost by parts (cherubic-georgian 478 to 479 of 482,
    a true note that counts where the fermata had held its place; pascha-christ-is-risen-appalachian 325 to 326
    of 328, the last note of the bass staff, which is both voices' again where the fermata under it had been the
    lower voice's note), and 5 notes that are not there fewer by the count (carol-gladly-sing, cherubic-georgian
    and its second scan, cherubic-znamenny, hierarchical-ye-holy-martyrs). Ten notes on fermatas go in all; the
    other changes are the voice of a note and one length (a half note under a fermata on a thick copy, which had
    been outvoted in its column). Staff by staff 1 note is gained (cherubic-georgian) and 1 lost:
    cherubic-znamenny--s2, a thick copy, is set on its staves another way by the count once the fermata under
    its last chord is no note (two staves of two parts where it had four staves of one part each). Its notes by
    parts stay as they were. The fresh set: 6 pages differ, 22 notes that are not there fewer, each an engraved
    fermata by sight (in five pieces: 10 to 3, 12 to 5, 15 to 11, 5 to 3 and 5 to 3), and no note gained or
    lost.
  - **A filled head with a speck in it, beside the loop of its flag** (`symbols.SOLID_SPECK`, a rule for one
    case). extra-rejoice-o-virgin lacked two notes, one head: a filled head that two voices share, with a
    flagged stem up and a flagged stem down. The lower flag closes a room of paper with the stem and a staff
    line, and the print pressed a speck of paper out of the middle of the head. A filled head beside the room of
    its flag is known to the reader (`SOLID_LOOP`: the solid ink over the room is as high as a head), but the
    rows of the speck counted as rows of the room, the solid ink was then no head high, and the mark was a
    hollow head that a later step took for the trail of a flag; the loop of the upper flag stayed as a note of
    its own. The first form took every piece of closed paper under a twentieth of the box of a head for a speck.
    It changed 60 pages, gained 12 notes and lost 30 (2 and 10 on the clean copies; 2 and 4 on the fresh set):
    half of the inside of a hollow head on a line is as small on a small print. The census of every mark where
    that form acted on its 60 pages (713 marks) gave the shape of the one case: the closed paper of the mark is
    two pieces, a speck and a room; the mark is no wider than a head; and the speck lies inside the head, half a
    head's height from the rows of the room. The eleven other marks of that kind, each looked at, are four
    hollow heads that a ledger line or a staff line cuts, two signs, two letters and pieces of three clefs, and
    their small piece of paper lies at the large one (0.21 of a head's height from its rows at most, against
    0.52). Narrowed so, on the 60 pages: one page differs, the head is read in both voices and the false note is
    gone (extra-rejoice-o-virgin 252 to 254 of 254). Of the 6 pages of the fresh set that the first form
    changed, none differs.
  - **A head of a chord keeps a dot that is sure** (`symbols.DOT_SURE`, `music.CHORD_DOT`).
    liturgy-we-praise-thee lacked one note in each of its two scans. Its print sets three voices on the upper
    staff: a whole note, and under it a half note and a dotted half note at one stem. The music step makes the
    heads of a chord at one stem as long as each other (`music.CHORD_ONE`: a dot that is none, a flag that was
    missed), and it took the printed dot. The rule is worth its place: switched off on every scan page it gains
    6 notes and loses 13 (1 and 5 on the clean copies), on 25 pages. That reading names the 17 heads whose dot
    the rule takes, and each was looked at with the measures of its dot. Eight have a printed or drawn dot,
    eight a speck of a worn copy, a stroke or a piece of a thick staff line, and one stands at a pair of glued
    heads that is misread anyway. The first form asked for a dot whose shorter side is 0.28 staff spaces: it
    kept five dots, each a true one, and the second scan of the hymn itself then showed the same printed dot
    with a side of 0.27. The specks have sides of 0.23 at most, so that measure parts the two by a hair. The ink
    of the dot parts them more widely: a round speck holds 0.043 staff spaces squared at most and a dot 0.067 or
    more, and the specks that hold more ink (up to 0.063) are slim (0.59 times as wide as high at most, the dots
    0.74 or more). A dot is sure when it holds 0.055 staff spaces squared of ink, is round, and stands in a
    space, away from the rows of the staff lines. Four of the eight true dots are sure. Left out: two that a
    blurred copy ran into a taller blot, a wide one by hand, and one that a hand set on a staff line. Alone, on
    the 25 pages: 4 pages differ, four heads keep their dots, 2 notes gained and none lost by parts
    (liturgy-we-praise-thee 148 to 149 of 149; cherubic-voronkoff--s2, a copy by hand, 475 to 476); staff by
    staff 1 gained and 1 lost. In cherubic-voronkoff--s2 the dotted half note on its ledger line is right now,
    and the same head is read a second time (the line cuts its inside in two): that reading had stood for the
    note with a wrong length, counts as a note that is not there now, and pushes the line-up of the sheet. The
    fresh set, every page: none differs.
  - **A half note between two read heads of one stem** (`symbols.SLIT_BETWEEN`). In the second scan of
    liturgy-we-praise-thee the half note of that chord was lost: a grey smear of the scan half fills its inside,
    the closed paper that is left is a slit of half the size that a head from its inside needs (0.48 of the
    usual inside of the page, against 0.6), and its middle lies in the box of the whole note over it. The
    census, at the reader's own step, is every piece of closed paper that is not round or not closed between two
    read hollow heads one over the other in its column: 69 pieces on LitChoir's scan pages, 8 of them paper that
    is not quite round with no head read at its place. Three are slits that rise to the right as the inside of a
    printed half note does, and each is the middle head of three: this one, the same chord with the same smear
    in liturgy-holy-holy--s2, and a half note with a thick rim in blessed-be-zakkak, a clean score that is not
    in the count. That third head is made by the rule and refused by the next step: the stems of its chord stand
    as one upright line through the whole staff, the head from the slit is lower than a head, and the step for
    the paper where a tie crosses a bar line takes it (`symbols.BAR_MARK`). It stays lost and is on the list of
    what is left. The five others are no slits: the insides of whole notes in the chords of two heavy copies,
    and the loops of two clefs. Such a slit is the inside of the head between the two, from 0.3 of the usual
    inside on, and it may lie in their boxes. Alone: 2 pages differ, two heads are read anew, and the count does
    not move. In liturgy-we-praise-thee--s2 the new half note makes the chord rule take the dot of the dotted
    half note under it, which the rule before this one keeps: with both, 148 to 149 of 149. In
    liturgy-holy-holy--s2, a thick copy, the new head takes the dark edge of the smear for a dot and is a dotted
    half note where the page prints a half note: the head is right, its length is not, and the count shows one
    more note that is not there. The fresh set, every page: none differs.
  - Looked at, no rule. megalynarion-zakkak lacks three notes: a dotted half note under a slur in pencil that is
    read as a whole note, a quarter note that touches the beamed head of the other voice and is read as an
    eighth note, and a note of the bass inside a pencil ring that is read a step over the typed one. That head
    stands in its space as the note before it does: it is a candidate for the list of the notes by the page, to
    be looked at with the hymn's other scan. pascha-receive-short lacks two: two half notes a third apart that
    are glued at the ledger dash over the bass staff (the rule for such a pair, `DASH_PAIR`, reads filled heads
    only). pascha-christ-is-risen-appalachian lacks two after the fermata: a faint dotted half note whose ring
    is open, read from its stem as a quarter note a step low. trisagion-vitoshinsky reads every note and has two
    that are not there: the swollen middle of a bold slur with the arm of the bow as its stem, and one head too
    many in a smudged final chord. The wide flat marks that are read as notes on the clean pages were listed
    from the stored notes and looked at: the middle of a slur, the line under "rit.", ties and pieces of ledger
    lines, no one cause.
  - The four rules together, every scan page (`n369` against `n366`): 14 pages differ, the pages of the four
    rules, 7 notes gained and none lost by parts, 4 gained and 2 lost staff by staff (the thick copy and the
    copy by hand above). The software-made PDFs were read with the round's code, because a rule of the music
    step changed: each of the 141 pages reads as main reads it.
  - The full reading of every page, with the words (`w369v1` against `w366v1`): clean copies 22,080 of 22,649
    notes (97.5 %; 40 at 99 %; 21,946 of 22,606 as typed), 6 gained and none lost by parts, 4 and none staff by
    staff; 6,067 of 6,254 syllables (97.0 %; 6,003 of 6,259 as typed) and 4,298 of 4,527 whole words, none
    gained or lost; 230 notes that are not there (225 with the places that a hand changed apart), 3 fewer; by
    the count before round 78 they are 430 and 420. The base scored as typed gives the figures of round 79
    exactly (21,942 of 22,606 and 6,003 of 6,259). 34 clean scores read every printed note (565 lacking; 31
    before), 38 every syllable (187 lacking), 27 both (25), 35 have no note that is not there and 26 are whole
    in all three (24): extra-rejoice-o-virgin and liturgy-we-praise-thee are whole now, and the second scan of
    liturgy-we-praise-thee reads every note. 8 scores lack one to three notes (10), and 442 notes are lacking
    for 99 % of each, as before: the notes of this round stand on scores that were over 99 %. All scores 78,699
    of 89,863 (87.6 %; 94 at 99 %): 7 gained and none lost by parts, 4 and 2 staff by staff, and 2,535 notes
    that are not there, 3 fewer (4,629 by the count before round 78). One layout moves (cherubic-znamenny--s2,
    see the fermata above); no window and no count of the notes that no page prints moves. Syllables 25,557 of
    28,695 (89.1 %), none gained or lost. Scans 58,683 of 69,700 notes (84.2 %) and 19,904 of 22,590 syllables;
    thick copies 7,101 of 8,532, as before; copies by hand 29,502 of 38,519 and 12,075 of 14,356. The kit: 6,232
    of 6,901 by parts and 5,800 of 6,419 staff by staff (the head of extra-rejoice-o-virgin, which two voices
    share: 2 and 1). The fresh set, every page read with the words: 39,939 of 41,761 notes, 8,302 of 11,341
    syllables and 5,899 of 6,647 whole words, as before, and 785 notes that are not there (807 before).
- Round 79 (20 November 2026): a heavy sharp that is one blot of ink with its note, and the quarter rest of an
  older print that is an X.

  The round went on with what the clean copies lack. Two rules came of it, each from a census of every mark of
  its kind on LitChoir's scan pages, and each for prints that set their signs heavily.

  - What the clean copies lack. After round 78 they lack 574 printed notes in 54 scores: cherubic-lvovsky 99,
    extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov 51, nativity-kontakion 31, communion-lords-prayer 30. The lost notes of
    cherubic-lvovsky, a pale and heavy engraving, were looked at one by one on its four pages. They are of four
    kinds: the heads of two voices a second apart that touch (a hollow half note glued to a filled quarter note),
    a heavy sharp that is one blot with its head, two whole notes one over the other whose rims lie in the staff
    lines, and a whole note glued to the small head of the other voice. Counted over the sheets of all clean
    copies by where the other voice's note stands: of 100 typed notes a second from the other voice's note 22 are
    not read right (434 such notes), against 4.6 where a voice sings alone, 6.7 at a third and 8.4 further apart.
    The same picture gives another mark each time (one mark with two stems; a hollow head whose rim is too thin
    for a mark; a worn filled head that is a small mark; one hollow mark with two stems), so no rule came of it
    yet.
  - **A heavy sharp that is one blot with its head** (`symbols.SIGN_WHOLE`, `signs.WHOLE_SIGN`). A sharp whose
    bars are as thick as a head touches its head and is one mark with it, the whole sharp (`SIGN_GLUED` of an
    earlier round cuts the mark that the right stroke alone makes with the head). Such a mark is no head for its
    size: it was read as a beam, for the stroke and the stem that leave it downwards at its two ends, or as
    nothing, and the note was lost. The census, at the reader's own step, is every mark of LitChoir's scan pages
    that is larger than a head and from whose left half one stroke leaves it upwards and another downwards, each
    a short way: 243 marks. The 132 with a stem at the right were looked at: eleven are a sharp and its head, on
    five prints (cherubic-lvovsky 2, megalynarion-kievan 6, two scans of the lamentations 1 each,
    non-liturgical-praise-ye--s2 1). Their strokes stand 0.48 to 0.78 staff spaces apart, the right one higher (the
    strokes of a natural stand the other way round), and go 0.24 to 0.71 past the mark. The others are treble
    clefs (strokes 0.34 apart), the sharps of a key signature that touch each other, the end of a staff with its
    bar lines, hatching and letters by hand. The mark is cut at the head's stem where the stem goes down, and at
    its narrowest column between the sharp and the stem where the stem goes up at the right. Three things came
    of the first forms. The paper that the sharp closes in with the stem and the head lies in the box of the head:
    with it the head was read as a half note, and its middle lay a step low (megalynarion-kievan's D sharps
    were read as C sharps). That paper reaches the cut, and it is no part of the head now. The sharps of a key
    signature that touch were cut as a sharp and a head: a head holds a circle of ink (0.62 of a head's height
    or more in the eleven heads, 0.46 in the piece of the key signature), and the share of ink in the box does
    not part them (0.58 and 0.57). And on one page the upper bar of the sharp hides the top of its left stroke,
    so the step that reads the signs found no pair of strokes and the note was read without its sharp: the cut
    knows the sharp, and the head carries it to that step. The eleven heads are filled, and the circle of ink
    refuses a hollow one: it is a rule for filled heads. Alone, on every scan page: 7 pages differ, the pages of
    the eleven marks, 13 notes gained and none lost by parts and 11 and none staff by staff (cherubic-lvovsky 762
    to 766 of 861, two notes read anew and two by the line-up of the count; megalynarion-kievan 442 to 447;
    non-liturgical-praise-ye--s2 591 to 593; the two scans of the lamentations 1 each, where the head is both
    voices'). No page of the fresh set differs.
  - **The quarter rest of an older print that is an X** (`rests.CROSS`). nativity-troparion (three scans of one
    print) sets its quarter rest as an X on the middle line of the staff, with a short upright through the
    crossing. The staff lines close the paper between its arms, and the cells were read as whole notes and half
    notes: 8 notes that are not there on the three scans. The census is every piece of ink without the staff
    lines that stands in a staff and has a waist, wide at its top and at its bottom and narrow between: 753
    pieces on LitChoir's scan pages. 43 of them are of the size of the X, in the middle of a staff, with two legs
    at the bottom: the six rests, 22 signs of common time, 13 pairs of whole notes one over the other, a number
    and an eighth rest. The closed paper does not part them (the X closes three cells with the pieces of the
    staff lines between its arms, as two whole notes close their insides). Where the ink stands does: the waist
    of the C stands at the left of its box, and in the rows around the middle the sides of two whole notes fill
    the width of the box, where the X stands clear of both sides. The rule reads the six rests and no other
    piece. The rest stands on the middle line and names no voice, so it is not written into the music; the
    marks in it are no notes, and a note before it is not made longer. Alone, on every scan page: the 3 pages
    differ, 8 notes that are not there fewer (nativity-troparion 6 to 3, its second scan 6 to 4, its third 4 to
    1), none gained and 1 lost, by parts and staff by staff. The lost one, on the second scan: the half note
    before the rest touches the filled head of the other voice over it, and the heavy copy nearly closed its
    hole (0.043 of its mark, against 0.049 for a hollow head of that page). It had been read as a quarter note and made a half note by the way to the mark in the rest that
    was read as the next note; with the rest read, it stays the quarter note that its head was read as. The
    rule stays, and the head is on the list of what is left. No page of the fresh set differs.
  - Looked at, no rule. Two more causes of the same print (nativity-troparion): a heavy sharp before a chord of
    two voices that closes paper with the chord and the staff lines, so that the sharp and the chord are one
    piece of thick ink (the middle of the sharp is read as a filled head with the strokes as its stems, and on
    two scans the chord is not read: one place on three scans); and a thick beam that begins along a staff
    line, whose pieces between the lines are read as heads, the first with the stem of its own note (5 notes that
    are not there; `BEAM_PIECE` asks for a piece with no stem of its own). Five clean scores that lack 2 to 6
    notes were looked at place by place, each with causes of its own: two half notes of two voices that one
    print draws as thin open hooks whose rims are the staff lines (trisagion-obleschuk), a head on ledger lines
    a step off and the first note after the key signature (nativity-god-is-with-us), a filled head and a
    hollow one a second apart that are read as one head of two voices, and two whole notes under a marker
    (liturgy-one-is-holy--s2), a heavy print (pascha-christ-is-risen-romanian), whole notes under the staff of
    a faint print (orthros-apichima). On extra-come-bless a half note on a line that its print draws as two
    bows about the stem is read twice, and the numbers of a time signature in the middle of a staff and a sign
    of cut time are read as notes.
  - The full reading of every page, with the words (`w366v1` against `w363v1`): clean copies 22,074 of 22,649
    notes (97.5 %; 40 at 99 %; 21,942 of 22,606 as typed), 4 gained and 1 lost by parts, 2 and 1 staff by
    staff; 6,067 of 6,254 syllables (97.0 %; 6,003 of 6,259 as typed) and 4,298 of 4,527 whole words, none
    gained or lost; 233 notes that are not there (228 with the places that a hand changed apart), 9 fewer; by
    the count before round 78 they are 433 and 423. The base scored as typed gives the figures of round 78
    exactly (21,939 of 22,606 and 6,003 of 6,259). 31 clean scores read every printed note (571 lacking), 38
    every syllable (187 lacking), 25 both, 35 have no note that is not there and 24 are whole in all three, as
    after round 78: the scores that the rules change lack more than these notes. 10 scores lack one to three
    notes, and 442 notes are lacking for 99 % of each. All scores 78,692 of 89,863 (87.6 %; 94 at 99 %): 13
    gained and 1 lost by parts, 11 and 1 staff by staff, and 2,538 notes that are not there, 9 fewer (4,633 by
    the count before round 78). No layout, no window and no count of the notes that no page prints moves.
    Syllables 25,557 of 28,695 (89.1 %), none gained or lost. Scans 58,676 of 69,700 notes (84.2 %) and 19,904
    of 22,590 syllables; thick copies 7,101 of 8,532 (megalynarion-kievan and non-liturgical-praise-ye--s2
    are thick copies); copies by hand 29,501 of 38,519 and 12,075 of 14,356. The kit: 6,230 of 6,901 by parts
    and 5,799 of 6,419 staff by staff, as before. The fresh set: no page differs by either rule (39,939 of
    41,761 notes, 8,302 of 11,341 syllables, 5,899 of 6,647 whole words, 807 notes that are not there).

- Round 78 (20 November 2026): a beam that runs into a staff line, the pieces of a beam that were read as notes,
  and a count of the notes that are not there that counts one head once.

  The round went on with the wrong lengths of the clean copies and with the notes that the reader writes there
  and that are not there. Two rules for the notes came of it, each from a census of every mark of its kind on
  LitChoir's scan pages, and a change of the count: nearly half of the notes that the count had called not there
  are printed heads that it counted for a second part.

  - **A beam that runs into a staff line** (`symbols.THIN_BEAM_HALF`). The wrong lengths of the clean copies by
    the sheets staff by staff are 185: 30 eighth notes read as quarters (cherubic-lvovsky 12,
    extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov 10), 17 quarters read as eighths, 15 dotted half notes read as half notes
    and 14 half notes read as quarters. An eighth note that is read as a quarter has a beam that the reader does
    not see. The census is every pair of neighbouring stems of LitChoir's scan pages whose straight line from
    stem end to stem end is ink or hidden in a staff line on 0.7 of the way or more, 3,449 pairs, each with the
    typed lengths of its notes. The notes that are typed shorter than a quarter and read as a quarter or longer
    hang on pairs of four kinds: a line that is hidden on too much of its way (36), a line that the reader sees
    and refuses (35), a line with gaps (44) and a line that misses a stem end (8). The first kind has a shape. A
    thin beam that slants crosses a staff line, and where its end lies in the line the reader sees ink on less
    of the way than a beam must show (0.6). 34 pairs are hidden so at one stem only. The eleven true beams
    among them rise or fall by 0.11 to 0.21 of their length, their ink lies straight on the line (the middle of
    the ink within 0.16 staff spaces of it), and they are seen on 0.4 of the way or more. The others are level:
    two stems that end at one staff line have that line between their ends, and a tie or a ledger line at one
    of them is the ink (five such pairs, straight too, none a beam); or their ink only touches the edge of the
    way, a slur or the next staff line. One true beam of the census is left: it lies level along its staff line
    (extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov). Alone, by the reading of the notes on every scan page, the first form
    changed seven pages where the census had named six: 12 notes gained and 1 lost. The lost one was a pair that
    the typed lengths had given no label, because one of its notes is not there: beside a note by hand a blot
    is read as a head with a short stem, and the line from that stem's end to the end of the note's own stem,
    which rises by 2.5 of its length, runs along the stem. No beam of the clean copies rises by more than 0.65,
    and the rule asks that now. Second form, on the seven pages: six differ, 12 notes gained and none lost, by
    parts and staff by staff alike (cherubic-lvovsky 760 to 762 of 861, nativity-forefeast-apolytikion 99 to
    101, cherubic-kastorsky and its second scan 625 to 629 each). No page of the fresh set differs.
  - A second census, of the thin lines that the reader sees and refuses because the line goes on past the last
    stem of its run, gave no rule: counted as beams, 17 notes would be gained and 56 true quarter notes lost. A
    slur over three notes passes the stem ends as a beam does (holy-saturday-koinonikon holds such slurs within
    0.1 staff spaces of the stem ends).
  - **One printed head counts once** (the wide scorer, outside git; `LENT=0` counts as before). lazarus-koinonikon
    reads every note and every syllable of its page and showed 3 notes that are not there. They are the upper
    notes of the bass's octaves under a note that the tenor holds: the count splits a staff of two parts by
    the top and the bottom note of each chord, the upper note went to the tenor, the bass's typed chord took
    the same head from the staff as right (one head for two parts, as a unison is), and the tenor's copy of it
    stood as a note that is not there. The count's notes that are not there held three kinds of such heads.
    The top or the bottom note of a chord of one voice that the split gives to the other part while its own
    part counts it as right: 67 on all scores. A note that one voice sings alone, which the split gives to
    both parts as a unison, and which is lined up with a typed note in one part and with none in the other,
    where that part rests or holds: 1,500. And a mark that is lined up with a typed note in neither part,
    which counted twice: 555, counted once now. The count takes them off after the line-ups are made and
    changes no line-up, and the choice between the ways to set a score on its staves is made as before (such
    a head still counts as a mistake there: without that one copy by hand would have changed its way and lost
    2 notes and 2 syllables by the count). On the reading of round 77 no other figure moves, on any score of
    LitChoir or of the fresh set: the notes right, the syllables, the layouts and the windows are the same.
    The notes that are not there: 4,699 to 2,577 on all scores, 447 to 245 on the clean copies (437 to 240
    with the places that a hand changed apart; the sheets staff by staff had shown 231), 3,918 to 807 on the
    fresh set, whose answers are exact: one of its pieces showed 321 where 15 marks are not there, because a
    second voice that is typed with two notes was given the lower note of every chord of its staff. Of the clean scores of
    round 77, 34 have no note that is not there (30 by the count before) and 24 are whole in all three (23):
    lazarus-koinonikon is new. cherubic-lvovsky showed 68 notes that are not there and has 25,
    extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov 40 and 10, extra-come-bless 26 and 7.
  - **The pieces of a beam are no notes** (`symbols.BEAM_PIECE`, `without_beam_pieces`, after the flags and the
    beams of the stems are counted). extra-rejoice-o-virgin read every note but two and showed one note that is
    not there: a piece of a beam between two stems, read as a head with no stem. A flat mark on the line between
    the ends of two neighbouring stems that both carry a flag or a beam is a piece of that beam, of a flag or of
    a slur over the two stem ends. A first census from the stored notes paired the stems wrongly (the upper head
    of a chord stores the stem as it was when the head was made, with an early end), so the census was made at
    the reader's own step: 94 marks of LitChoir's scan pages lie on such a line, and the 44 flat ones (lower than
    0.7 of the page's heads) were each looked at. 42 have no stem that goes the way of the pair: 31 pieces of
    beams and flags and the cells of paper under them, 7 slurs across two stems, 3 pieces of a sign of common
    time by hand beside the clef and a letter of a heading. None is a note (the count had called 2 right by
    chance). Of the two flat marks with such a stem one is a small head by hand, so the rule leaves a mark that
    has a stem of its own that way. True heads on such a line are 0.74 of the page's heads high (one, by hand)
    and 0.79 or more; from 0.7 to 0.9 heads and pieces stand mixed, and the rule takes the part that holds no
    head. Alone over the first rule, on every scan page: 35 pages differ, 32 notes that are not there fewer, 9
    notes gained and 4 lost by parts, 6 gained and none lost staff by staff. The 4 are one score by hand
    (blessed-be-bortniansky--s2, 126 to 122 by parts and 126 to 127 on its sheet): the thick piece of a slur
    that went had been read as a G of the bass and had held the line-up of that part. The gains are lengths
    that the false marks had outvoted in their columns: extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov 446 to 447 and
    trisagion-arabic 303 to 304 on the clean copies, cherubic-kastorsky and its second scan 629 to 631,
    trisagion-oldziey 229 to 231, blessed-be-serbian--s2 140 to 141. extra-rejoice-o-virgin has no note that is
    not there now (252 of 254). No page of the fresh set differs.
  - Looked at, no rule. The clean scores that lack 1 to 3 notes or show 1 to 3 notes that are not there were
    looked at place by place: eight places, eight causes. The swollen middle of a slur read as a head and a blot
    on a chord of three hollow heads on ledger lines (trisagion-vitoshinsky, pg0135); two marks on a wavy pencil
    line (wedding-alleluia-grechaninov, pg0274); the loop of the flag of a head that two voices share

    scan pages, each read note with the count's verdict, parted nothing. The quarter rest of an older engraving
    that is read as notes (nativity-troparion) was not begun.

- Round 77 (20 November 2026): the arc of a ring that is no stem, and the speck of paper where two filled heads
  meet.

  The round went on with the clean scores that are nearly whole, and with the notes that the reader writes on
  the clean copies and that are not there: each of the 238 on the sheets staff by staff was sorted by its kind
  and looked at. Two rules came of it, each from a census of every mark of its kind on LitChoir's scan pages.
  What had no rule is listed at the end.

  - **A stem is straight** (`symbols.STEM_ARC`). The arc of a pencil ring around a note, of a bracket by hand
    before a remark or of an arrow is a thin stroke that goes on upright for a few staff spaces, and the finder
    that reads a head from its stem (`symbols.thin_heads`) read the arc as a stem with a small head at its end.
    Round 76 left these heads: no measure of the head parts the false ones. The stroke does. It is followed
    row by row from the middle of the stem, as far as slim ink goes and past the stem's end too, without the
    rows at the head. Where that way bends off a straight line by 0.35 staff spaces or more as one bow, the
    stroke is an arc and the head is not read. One bow: a parabola fits the way within half of the bend, and
    each half of the way bends to the same side as the whole. Two earlier measures did not part. The stem as
    the reader has it, from its end to the head, is too short: an arc and the stem of a hand differ by less
    than the trace wanders there. And a way that is followed into the head reads the rim of the head, a rest or
    a number beside it. The census is every head read from its stem on LitChoir's scan pages, 3,217, each with
    the count's verdict: 22 ways bend so, 7 on the clean copies (rings by pencil and by pen, brackets, an
    arrow, a number in a ring) and 15 on copies by hand, each looked at. One of the 22 is a true note whose
    stem a hand bent; it was read with a wrong length before, and no count moves by it. The true notes whose
    way is one bow bend by 0.31 staff spaces at most, and by 0.17 on the clean copies; two false ones of clean
    copies at 0.29 and 0.30 are left. Alone, by the reading of the notes on every scan page: the 17 pages of
    the census differ and no other, 1 note is gained on all scores (a copy by hand) and none lost, 2 gained
    and none lost staff by staff, and 15 notes that are not there are gone, 8 of them on six clean scores
    (nativity-canon-ode-5 1 to 0, orthros-apichima 1 to 0, holy-saturday-koinonikon 2 to 1,
    hierarchical-xenia-prokeimenon--s2 11 to 10, orthros-doxology-t6--s2 13 to 11, pascha-angel-cried--s2 14 to
    12). On two scans of one antiphon by hand the brackets around a "3" had been lined up with typed notes of a
    recitation; with the brackets gone the count sets 3 more typed notes apart as recited, and no figure of
    those two scores moves. No page of the fresh set differs.
  - **The speck of paper where two filled heads meet** (`symbols.HEADS_MEET`). Two filled heads a third apart
    at one stem touch, and between the two heads and the stem a speck of paper is closed in. The piece of ink
    is cut evenly into its heads, the speck falls to one of them, and that head was read as a half note at the
    place of the speck, a step from its own: a wrong length, and on most pages a wrong pitch too. Both
    mistakes of forerunner-nativity-apolytikion were such specks (one note read wrongly, one not there). The speck lies
    at the cut and beside the stem; the inside of a half note lies in the middle of its head. A part whose
    inside has its middle at most 0.2 staff spaces from the edge where the next part of its piece begins, a
    filled one, and 0.15 of the part's width or more beside the part's middle, at the side of a stem of the
    part, is a filled head, and its place is that of its ink. The rule had three forms. The first asked for
    the place at the cut and for two parts as high as a head (0.85 staff spaces), from a census of the stored
    notes (3,779 hollow heads whose box ends where the box of another head of its column begins): on the
    pages of that census 7 notes gained on the clean copies and none lost, and a copy by hand changed. The
    height was measured with the staff space of the page, which is a little smaller there than that of the
    mark's own staff, and two half notes by hand whose piece was cut into three parts passed by a hair. The
    second form took the measure that the looked-at marks share: the speck lies beside the middle of its
    part, at the side of the stem, and a true inside lies in the middle. It gained 6 of the 7: the stem of the
    seventh pair is one that the reader finds only at a later step. Without the stem the rule would reach
    that pair, and the census was made again, at the reader's own step and on every scan page, to see what
    else it would reach: 1,103 hollow parts with a filled part of their piece that begins where they end; the
    insides of 55 lie within 0.2 staff spaces of the cut, and 14 of them 0.15 of the width or more beside the
    middle (0.16 to 0.28; the others 0.13 at most). Seven of the 14 have a stem at that side, all on clean
    copies, and each is the speck of such a pair (one more than the stored notes had named). The seven
    others have no stem there: three numbers of time signatures, a clip, a blot and a stack of whole notes
    by hand, and the one true pair (transfiguration-troparion--s3, which keeps its mistake). The rule keeps
    the stem. The stored notes had shown 17 insides near a cut where the step itself has 55: the parts of a
    sharp, of a flat and of a number are marks of the kind of a head at that step, and a stored note carries
    a stem that a later step gave it. One more shape is in the rule, in its third form: on one page the
    speck was made a part of its own, a hollow head between the two filled ones, a note that is not there. A
    hollow part between two filled parts of its piece, in its column and at its stem, whose middles are a
    third apart (0.85 to 1.25 staff spaces) with the inside midway, is no head: three heads a second apart
    do not stand in one column. Of the 13 hollow parts of LitChoir's pages with a filled part over and under
    them, one hangs on a stem with both, that speck; the others are the cells of the sharps of a key
    signature and the bowl of a flat. The first form of this half asked only for the two neighbours, half a
    staff space away each, and would have taken those cells.
    Alone, by the reading of the notes on every scan page, over the reading with the first rule: 5 pages differ,
    the pages of the seven specks and of the one between two heads, and no other; 7 notes gained on five clean
    scores and none lost, by parts and staff by staff alike (forerunner-nativity-apolytikion 169 to 170 of 170
    and no note that is not there, liturgy-mercy-of-peace--s2 394 to 395 of 402, liturgy-what-shall-i-render 315
    to 316 of 366, extra-rejoice-o-virgin-rachmaninov 444 to 446 of 498, transfiguration-troparion--s3 284 to 286
    of 312). No page of the fresh set differs. It is a rule for prints: on the copies by hand and the thick
    copies no part passes all of it.
  - **Looked at, no rule.** A note of the melody that touches the ison's breve under it is not read: LitChoir's
    pages hold one such place (pascha-hypakoe), too few to set a rule on, and the fresh set's chant pieces lack
    about 240 notes so. A pencil ring cannot be taken off the page, because its ink crosses the notes and has
    no one shape; its arc is no stem now, and the word under a ring is still read as one letter
    (nativity-canon-ode-9). cherubic-lvovsky reads 12 eighth notes as quarters: hairline beams that lie along
    the staff lines, faint broken stems and narrow flags, several causes on one print. The 238 notes that are
    not there on the sheets of the clean copies were sorted by kind (61 hollow heads with a stem, 54 filled
    quarter notes, 34 whole notes, 32 heads read from a stem, 17 without a place on the page, and smaller
    kinds), and no kind is one cause. They hold: the quarter rest of an older engraving, shaped like an X
    (nativity-troparion's print in three scans, about 7 notes, and extra-come-bless), which `rests.py` does
    not read; reciting whole notes that the count calls not there; two whole notes that a pen
    crossed out (triodion-meatfare, candidates for the list of the notes that a hand changed); a clef inside a
    bracket (wedding-service, 4); the word "Hold" written into the staff by hand (pascha-hypakoe on two scans,
    4); the numbers of a time signature in the middle of a staff and two signs of cut time; two sharps of a
    key signature (pascha-exapostilarion); pencil rings and a paperclip.
  - The full reading of every page, with the words (`w359v1` against `w356v1`): clean copies 22,067 of 22,649
    notes (97.4 %; 40 at 99 %; 21,935 of 22,606 as typed), 7 gained and none lost; 6,067 of 6,254 syllables
    (97.0 %; 6,003 of 6,259 as typed) and 4,298 of 4,527 whole words, none gained or lost; 447 notes that are
    not there (437 with the places that a hand changed apart), 9 fewer; staff by staff 8 gained and none lost.
    The base scored as typed gives the figures of round 76 exactly (21,928 of 22,606 and 6,003 of 6,259). 31
    clean scores read every printed note (578 lacking), 38 every syllable (187 lacking), 25 both, 30 have no
    note that is not there, and 23 are whole in all three: forerunner-nativity-apolytikion and
    nativity-canon-ode-5 are new. 10 scores lack one to three notes, and 449 notes are lacking for 99 % of

    lost staff by staff, and 16 notes that are not there fewer (4,699). The count of the drawn notes is 3
    smaller, by the typed notes of a recitation that the two brackets had stood for (5,865 typed notes that no
